- **Predicate direction**: A (Colorado) is adjacent to B (New Mexico); neither contains the other.  
- **Result**: The only topological relation that matches a shared boundary without interior overlap is **touches**.  
Suggested predicate: **touches**

---

**BRANCH 2: “Overlap vs. Touch” Disambiguation**  
- **Step 1 (equals)**: No textual or geometric evidence of identical extents; the states are distinct.  
- **Step 2 (within/contains)**: Colorado is not fully inside New Mexico, nor does New Mexico fully enclose Colorado.  
- **Step 3 (overlaps)**: The phrase “meeting at a geometric edge” indicates no interior intersection; the polygons only share a boundary.  
- **Step 4 (touches)**: The shared edge is the definition of a touch.  
- **Direction**: A→B is symmetric for touches, but we confirm A (Colorado) touches B (New Mexico).  
Suggested predicate: **touches**

- **Rule application**: Since the shared geometry is a line (boundary) and not an area, the relation is **touches**.
